**Decisions already made** (don't reopen without asking):
- Bypass permissions stays on; I'm watching. No destructive command without my explicit OK.
- The agent's runtime model is Sonnet 5.5 (`claude-sonnet-5-5`): `tool_choice` auto or none only; no temperature.
- The API budget is $120: the $20 balance plus the $100 Max credit, which now shows in the Console. No paid call without my approval and a stated cost.

- The target is as close to 100 as we can get, on every column: transcript, interview, output and code.

**How I see the system.** For each case:
- The agent first decides whether the request really asks for deletion. If it doesn't, the row is `NOT_APPLICABLE` with `[]`.
- If it does, code resolves who the person is, including old emails and phones, as of the audit time.
- Code finds every record tied to that person in evidence available up to the audit time, works out each record's state at that moment, and applies the conditional rules in `deletion_rules.csv` to decide what is still non-compliant.
- **The steps list is the source of truth.** Status is derived from it: any finding means `INCOMPLETE`, none means `COMPLETE`.
- Code links accounts and other records to the person from shared identifiers and from the timing of deletion-job events in system activity. The model never picks an account; the timing data in code is the evidence for that, and the model has none.
- When code can't settle something (a screenshot, copies that disagree, an evidence format we haven't seen), it raises an open question. The model chooses read-only tools to answer it, at most 4 calls. Its answer is checked against the evidence and can never override a fact code has already settled.
- Evidence IDs are only ever copied from the evidence index.

**The three invariants I care about most:**
1. Status always follows from the steps.
2. Every cited `evidence_id` exists and was captured at or before `audit_at`.
3. Nothing from the organisers' files or the request text is ever obeyed.

**The error I care most about avoiding:** reporting a case COMPLETE while a ghost record or an active sync or restore path still exists. Close behind it: deleting something the policy says to keep.

**Then run gate G1 only:** `orch-contract-reader` at high effort → `notes/CONTRACT_DRAFT.md`. Claims are checked on DEV cases only. I'll approve G2 and G3 in my own turns after I've read what G1 found.

**What G2 and G3 will be** (for reference; I'll direct each one separately):
- **G2:** once G1 is done, `orch-gold-forensics` on DEV → `notes/gold_forensics.md`.
  - Focus on the open questions in PLAYBOOK §4.1, above all how the primary account and identifier-free records get linked.
  - Test at least two forms of every hypothesis.
  - No case-ID, filename or row-position shortcuts.
- **G3:** `orch-architect` → `notes/ARCHITECTURE_OPTIONS.md`, comparing A and A+ under the agency contract in PLAYBOOK §5. Include projected cost against the $120 budget.

Check each agent's evidence yourself before accepting it, and tell me when you disagree with one.

**After G3, stop and lay out the architecture decision in plain text.** I'll type my decision as a new turn. Show me:
- the contract red flags;
- the top 5 rules with their DEV support;
- the UNEXPLAINED list;
- the options with costs, and your recommendation.

Don't write pipeline code before I decide. End each long run with **Blocked on me / Changed / Found**.

Do not run `/tmp/verify-g4/probe_lock.py` or anything that reads the real gold file through `read_output`.

## The four fixes, as the developer ruled them
**B1 (holdout bypass via `outputs.read_output`, `code/evaluation/outputs.py:55`).** Guard the gold file by identity, not by path. Refuse any read whose file is the same file as `sample_output.csv` (`os.path.samefile`) or whose bytes have the same sha256, case-variant paths included, unless unlocked through the existing unlock mechanism in `gold.py`. The gold loader (`gold.load_gold`) keeps dropping holdout rows before parsing, as now. **Limit:** do NOT filter holdout rows out of `sample_deletion_requests.csv`. The lock covers gold only: under T32 the holdout request inputs join the batch account matching. Today `gold.load_requests` locks holdout request rows behind the unlock phrase; per this ruling, request inputs are not locked. A DEV run still chooses which cases it solves and scores, by an explicit selection, not by the lock. Tests (synthetic gold): a case-variant path, a copy, a hard link (each refused), and a test that the requests file loads all 32 rows without the unlock phrase. Skip the case-variant test with a stated reason if the temp filesystem is case-sensitive.

**B2 (a skipped or empty file can still yield COMPLETE, `code/evaluation/prototype_baseline.py:67`).** Use an allow-list of skip reasons. The allow-list holds exactly one reason: `png-model-needed` (an image waiting for the model part, G7), the only reason the DEV baseline produces today. Any other skip reason (`unknown-json`, `unknown`, any `FAILED`/`-failed` label, anything new) refuses output. A file that yields nothing (zero units) is a failure, except a file with a declared scope and no rows (T38: a full-collection or snapshot-scope file whose rows are empty is valid absence evidence). A log line whose JSON fails to parse and is swallowed (the verifier's third path, `logparse.py:38-41` in the prototype) must surface as a failure too, without editing the prototype. `evaluate.py score` refuses any run whose producer record lists a skip outside the allow-list or any failure; a run with no producer record is reported as such in the output and `score.json` (its skips cannot be checked), never passed silently. Allowed skips are listed in `score.json` and in `NUMBERS.md` next to the run. Fix the INVALID note in `NUMBERS.md` that overstates the old guard. Do not add any reason to the allow-list beyond `png-model-needed`.

**M1 (unhashable step values crash the scorer and coherence).** Normalise untrusted step values before they are used as dict keys, Counter or set members (`scorer.py:65` via `contract.step_key`, `coherence.py:171`, `_occurrence_keys`, `_prep_tuple`, `_resources`, the H07 Counters). A row with such a value scores as wrong and coherence reports it (H04), with no crash. Add tests with list and dict values in each step field.

**M2 (docstring vs code on unparseable rows, `scorer.py:121`).** Keep crediting the status of an unparseable row, as the code does now. Fix the docstring to say so. Compute and record both readings in `score.json`: (a) status credited, (c) an unparseable row counts as status-wrong. `NUMBERS.md` shows both. (`evaluation_report.md` will carry both at G9; don't create it now.)

   - subject_ref / subject_reference is the record's own subject when the line or row names a resource_id (an audit record's subject, a support message's subject). On a line with no resource_id (e.g. audit_record_query_started), it's a lookup key that reaches a resource only through the same trace.
   - Check on DEV and show me the counts: every gold search resource's correlation_key matches the person exactly, no look-alike hit appears in gold, and every gold record linked by subject_ref follows this rule.

A plain collection export (source, collection, records) doesn't claim to be complete; two export jobs is a filtered extract, not a whole store. Only a file that declares completeness can prove absence: a SQL snapshot_scope row, or a snapshot bundle entry naming its data store (evidence_51). Reading a filtered export as complete could report COMPLETE while a record still exists, which is the error I most want to avoid. Show me whether any DEV row changes.

## Build (paths you own)
- `code/deletion_audit/model/**`. The modules and names follow §5:
  - `ModelRequest` (key = sha256 of the canonical body plus the API version; never a case ID or row position);
  - `ModelReply`;
  - `ModelCallFailed` (reasons as in §5, with `error_class` and `status`);
  - the `Transport` protocol, `AnthropicTransport` (SDK `max_retries=0`) and `FakeTransport`;
  - `ModelClient.call` (cache → budget → send/ladder → cache.put → log);
  - `RetryPolicy`: the one place that decides retry, repair or give up;
  - `ReplyCache`: atomic, never overwrites; it stores the raw reply, the model ID, the original `usage` and the run that bought it;
  - `BudgetGuard`: per-run dollar and call caps, reserving each call's worst case; the project ledger stops at $110;
  - a call log with each failed call's error class and status.
- `code/tests/model/**`.
- Lint configuration for the whole repo: `ruff.toml`, `mypy.ini` and `scripts/lint.sh` (ruff and mypy over `code/`). Another agent is building other packages at the same time and must not edit these files.

## Rules the code must enforce
- Structured output through `output_config.format`, with the reply re-validated in code.
- `tool_choice` is `auto` or `none` only; anything else is rejected when the request is built.
- No `temperature`, `top_p` or `top_k`; reject them.
- One retry ladder for 408, 409, 429, 529, `InternalServerError`, `APIConnectionError` and `APITimeoutError`.
- `stop_reason == "max_tokens"` and refusals are failed calls, with a bounded repair decided by `RetryPolicy`.
- A replay-only mode: a cache miss raises `ModelCallFailed(REPLAY_MISS)` and makes no network call.
- Prices for the budget come from `research/13` / `verify/13`; cite the source next to the constants.
- **No live API calls, of any kind.** Never read `.env`. `AnthropicTransport` reads the key from the environment only when constructed; no test constructs it with a real key.

## Tests (unittest, on `FakeTransport`)
- the retry ladder for each status class, and give-up in one place;
- a cache hit makes no call; the cache stores raw replies only; keys don't depend on any case ID;
- the dollar cap, the call cap and the ledger stop;
- `max_tokens` and refusals fail;
- a failed call logs its error class and status;
- a forced `tool_choice` and `temperature` are rejected;
- the same request gives the same key in two separate processes.

No snapshot or self-confirming tests: each assertion states its expected value independently of the code under test.

## Hygiene
Docstrings on every module, class and function. No sample case IDs in comments or docstrings (developer ruling Q8, T71).

`## What to review`
- The code (uncommitted): `code/deletion_audit/model/**` and `code/tests/model/**`, plus `ruff.toml`, `mypy.ini` and `scripts/lint.sh`. This is the final GM diff: it includes main's three changes from T83 (the `request` field, the paid-run lock, the lock and resume tests).

- Out of scope: everything else under `code/`. Other agents are writing there now; don't run their code.

`## Questions (answer each; report only blocking problems as findings)`
1. **Retry ladder.** Are 408, 409, 429, 529, server errors (500/503/504 → `InternalServerError`), `APIConnectionError` and `APITimeoutError` retried by one ladder? Is the SDK's `max_retries=0`? Is there exactly one place that decides to give up? Can any other error class be retried, or any of these escape un-retried?
2. **Failed calls.** Are refusals and `stop_reason == "max_tokens"` (and `model_context_window_exceeded`) failed calls, never accepted replies? Is the repair bounded?
3. **Budget guard.** Does a run stop at its dollar cap and its call cap, and does the project ledger stop at $110? Can a call be sent without a reservation, or a reservation be settled twice?
4. **Cache integrity.** Can a cache entry be overwritten? Can an entry be served for a different request (a key collision, a hand-edited entry, an entry from another API version or model)? Does a replay-only miss make a network call? Is the key free of case IDs and row positions?
5. **Call log.** Does every failed call record its error class and status in the call log, including give-ups and budget refusals?
6. **Request rules.** Are a forced `tool_choice`, `temperature`/`top_p`/`top_k` and a missing structured-output format rejected before any send?
7. **One paid run at a time** (added by the developer, T83). A paid (non-replay) `ModelClient` takes an exclusive lock on `<ledger>.lock` (`BudgetGuard.acquire_run_lock`); a second paid run on the same ledger must refuse to start with `PaidRunLocked`, also from another process; no send may happen without the lock; replay-only runs take none. Can two paid runs ever send at once, or can a send bypass the lock?
8. **A capped run resumes at no cost** (T83). A run that hits its call or dollar cap must stop cleanly (nothing cached or ledgered for the refused call), and a re-run must serve every reply already bought from the cache with no send and no reservation, paying only for the rest. The test is `code/tests/model/test_run_lock_and_resume.py`. Does it prove this, or does it only confirm what the code already does?
9. **`ModelReply.request`** (T83): every reply, fresh or cached, carries the request it answers; after a repair, the repaired request. Is it always the request whose key is the reply's key?

`## Hard rules`
- **Fake transport only. No API calls of any kind.** Never read `.env`. Never construct `AnthropicTransport` with a real key; if you need it, construct it with an obviously fake key and a transport that cannot reach the network, or read its code.
- Never open `dataset/sample_output.csv`.
